EXPLICIT STATES (state-to-state reasoning; review-shaped states) — TARGET IDEAS:1351; PURPOSE 1374.\n- Exists: no state markers; State dataclass is mechanical; NOTEs (≤10 FIFO) are the only model-owned narrative state; bootstrap_corpus already renders PLAN/EXECUTE/REVIEW and META-FLOW text (the flows Rohin wants installed); classify() recognises prediction_revision/evidence_scope/stopping/memory_use strata by keywords; SELF_EVAL/CONTRAST regexes in efficiency_markers are the seed of a review detector.\n- Build: harness-driven state boundaries ([STATE k] plan / execute / review): inject \"[REVIEW] write your review of this state\" at state end and pin that REVIEW NOTE as a LAST REVIEW field at the head of state k+1 (render_context change); ledger rows get state_id and phase; _pathway exemplar becomes plan→execute→review; per-state ritual/rehearsal metrics (state_instances); a review-shape detector (where I went wrong / next time / generalises / scope) as the quantitative proxy for Rohin's qualitative deliverable (\"SEE strong self-reflective text\"). Whether structure is forced by prompt or only by the pretrained adapter is a design choice; the bootstrap success criterion assumes unprompted.\n- Time: 2–3 ED code (shared with B1 as the v7 stream) + 1 ED for detectors/per-state metrics; smoke 2 h; first life 46 h. Any render_context change invalidates the q fields of the existing bootstrap corpora (see B9).\n- Judged by: review-marker counts per state/cycle (new instrument, seeded from efficiency_markers regexes — regex counts are noisy per SEQ-005); contrast/self-eval counts; ritual onset; paired probes; harm; bootstrap success criterion (IDEAS:1372): unprompted review/plan text in the first 64 episodes with no parent and later ritual onset than controls (control onset 160–224 in 8/9).\n\nB3. k PARALLEL THINKING STREAMS MERGED BY THE CHILD (depth parallelism) — DIRECTION 1316, 1359; RULED 1399.\n- Exists: lockstep batching of k DIFFERENT programs (run_episodes_batch), each with its own State/tail, isolated within a batch; seeds keyed on (eid, tick) so k streams on the same eid would collide unless branch_id is folded in; vLLM n>1 unused (outputs[0]); grep confirms no [BRANCH]/[JOIN]/tree code anywhere in organism_v6 or gpu.\n- Build (harness-driven first, model-driven [BRANCH] later): fork k EpisodeDrivers on the same Episode with branch_id in the seed; run each to its budget; JOIN step: one prompt containing the k branch reviews (or truncated tails), the model writes one NOTE/plan that becomes the next state's head; ledger rows carry state_id, parent_state, branch_id. Sleep: select() groups by episode_id — branches on one eid would be merged into one episode's wins; needs grouping by (eid, instance, branch) or compiling the JOIN output as the pathway (ruling needed: \"sleep converges branches\" IDEAS:1360 vs 64_ \"raw branches remain lossless ledger records\", M13). Probe protocol decision: probe with k=1 (comparable to the 8-panel history) or k=train.\n- Time: 2–3 ED code + 1 ED metrics; throughput: k× generation and k× gym evaluations per situation (cgym ~0.5–1 s each) — at k=4 with wake-batch 8 = 32 concurrent streams per call; a 1024-episode life goes from ~46 h to roughly 100+ h unless episodes are cut to 512.\n- Judged by: paired probes (protocol fixed first); tokens-to-best across all k streams (efficiency instrument needs a total-token denominator — new); JS shift between cycles (the day-one gate: does behaviour change at all); ritual onset; new metrics: branches per state, outcome divergence across branches, joined-plan score vs best single branch at equal tokens; life-level harm.\n\nB4. RECURRENT CoT (return to and revise earlier reasoning across states) — RULED 1399.\n- Exists: HEAD carries ≤10 NOTEs + last 4 OPEN SURPRISES; TAIL last 14 chunks; RECALL is keyword retrieval (no by-id retrieval); nothing pins an earlier reasoning chunk for revision; bootstrap _OPENING (RECALL then NOTE restating the review) and _CONTRAST paths are exactly this shape in training data; classify() detects \"PREDICT … WRONG/REVIS\".\n- Build: mostly on top of B2: (a) REVIEW→next-state HEAD pinning; (b) exact retrieval by state_id (Ledger.recall variant, ~hours) so a \"RETURN TO state j\" pulls that state's plan+review verbatim into MIDDLE; (c) bootstrap strata that model revision (deviation experiments, contrast, opening — already in bootstrap_corpus); (d) detector: chunks referencing an earlier state (extend CONTRAST regex with state-id references).\n- Time: 1–2 ED beyond B2; 0.5 ED instrument.\n- Judged by: revision/contrast marker counts per cycle; prediction revision after surprise (backtracks); note Jaccard (should fall if notes reference specific earlier cases); paired probes; the second-derivative readout vs the baseline agent on a source-disjoint panel (the killer metric — programme target, not this paper).\n\nB5. PERSISTENCE GYM TASKS (exploit-gym style) — DIRECTION 1402; DEFINITION 1405.\n- Exists: CompilerGym deterministic per-ACT score, budget 16, base ~13 chunks/episode; brevity gate catches collapse to 1–2 chunks; rule game with 10 textbook rules and a quiz; cgym_eval --ref and --list-benchmarks; disjoint_panel_v1.json already records recipe_precheck/excluded_precheck (programs where the birth recipe has no headroom = natural \"recipe fails, search required\" programs); get_training_programs pools only cbench/chstone/mibench and re-derives from a live listing on resume (minor 8); backtracks and strategy switches already counted; no delayed-payoff task; no time-to-abandon metric (moot under no-DONE).\n- Build: (a) CompilerGym persistence split: programs where birth recipe ≤ base+0.02 but -Oz headroom ≥ 0.1, from datasets disjoint by source from both report panels (CPU precheck minutes; curriculum wiring ~0.5 ED; persist the shuffled list at launch); (b) rule game compound/sequence rules beyond the 10 (RULES list extension, ~0.5–1 ED) with the quiz gated behind a minimum number of probes; (c) a persistence score = f(eventual best, distinct ACTs after a plateau, continued attempts after non-improving outcomes) — 0.5 ED on efficiency_markers; (d) parent prompt variant that rewards continued changing attempts (text only); longer budgets on hard programs.\n- Time: ~2 ED total before lives.\n- Judged by: backtracks/episode, strategy switches, chunks-to-best (efficiency_markers; baseline: all fall to ~0 late-life in every arm), best score on the hard split, REJECTED_BREVITY counts per life (analysis_tables), life-level harm, per-program ON−OFF table (does per-program choice appear, i.e. does the recipe un-lock).\n\nB6. CLONE MERGE AT SLEEP WITH A SLEEP BARRIER (one child, many clones; speed parallelism) — DIRECTION 1393, 1362; RULED-PENDING 1396.\n- Exists: every gym life is an independent student with its own adapter dir and ledger (latest_adapter scans that life's sleep_* dirs); the merge exists only in classroom_round (pooled provenance rows → stratified mix → one v2.1 write → exams → transactional commit → hashed manifest), in-process on one GPU; ParentLedger/playbook is per lineage dir; close_backend() teardown escalation exists; compile_sleep produces untagged strings (no per-source provenance) whereas classroom rows carry evidence_ids/branch/round.\n- Build: N clone processes (one per GPU, same node — no cross-node link) sharing one child dir: each runs wakes into ledger_c{c}.jsonl, writes READY at the sleep boundary and polls for all N (or a coordinator); coordinator compiles over all clone ledgers with per-source tags (clone_id, env_id) — needs a provenance-carrying compile path (compile_native/classroom row shape, or compile_sleep extended), one write (writer choice = lineage decision), canary + disjoint-panel gate, commit; clones reload latest_adapter and resume (already how a life reloads after sleep). Multi-environment clones need run_life_v2 to accept a gym per clone (EpisodeDriver is gym-agnostic; run_life_v2 hardcodes CompilerGym). One playbook per child across classrooms (IDEAS:1362 gap) rides on ParentLedger keyed to the child dir.\n- Time: 3–5 ED (barrier + coordinator + provenance compile + multi-env launch) + 2-clone smoke (~4 h) + first merged run; ≤ 7 clones per node alongside a parent server.\n- Judged by: paired probes of the merged adapter on the 8-panel and a source-disjoint report panel vs (a) a single-clone life at matched episodes, (b) adapter-OFF, (c) the brief-only text-memory proxy; first derivative per wall-clock hour (the speed claim); life-level harm; classroom merge-test bar (IDEAS:227-230: each source's admitted lesson entered the pooled adapter, interface + anchors intact, pooled ≥ equal-size serial in stability); per-source ablation possible via provenance even though Rohin ruled attribution unnecessary (1399) — split hygiene (1396) still asks that \"did the test-gym clone's experience carry the gain\" be ablatable.\n\nB7. EVALUATOR CLASSROOM / EVALUATOR AGENT — DIRECTION 1393 item 4; RULED 1390 (always a baseline agent).\n- Exists: the paired probe protocol IS the evaluator (fixed 8-panel + disjoint 12-panel, seeded, BOOTSTRAP-only context, fresh ledger so probe rows never train); classroom_round exam phase (ON/OFF/PREV seeded rule-game exams); ritual/efficiency metrics on probe ledgers (analysis_tables ritual_probe); no evaluator agent; IDEAS:219-226 warns LLM-judge \"good reasoning\" is weak evidence; principle 1337: every claim traces to a world-side number.\n- Build: (a) minimal: a test-only environment set held out BY SOURCE, probed ON/OFF each cycle via run_probes_batch(eids=…) / probe_adapter --panel — hours; (b) an evaluator agent (parent-class model) scoring reflection quality on probe ledgers against a written rubric with prompt version logged, reported as secondary — 1–2 ED; (c) the second-derivative readout: a deployed agent learning on the test gym's training split with probes on a further-held-out split vs the baseline agent (adapter-OFF and text-memory) — the current life runner already has this shape; the missing piece is a fresh task family whose score rewards situation-dependent choice (IDEAS:1380 item 3: on cbench the gain ceiling is one recipe; -Oz 0.522).\n- Time: hours–1 ED plumbing; 1–2 ED evaluator agent; task-family choice is a design decision plus days.\n- Judged by: paired probes (first derivative); rate of improvement on the novel family, developed vs baseline (second derivative — IDEAS:1384 killer metric, programme target); efficiency markers and ritual ON vs OFF on probe ledgers; marker counts.\n\nB8. PROVENANCE GUARD (fail-closed clean-lineage / birth manifest) — Codex firewall COORD:813-846; STOP COORD:857-886; contamination rule IDEAS:1346.\n- Exists: free-text only — classroom manifest gym_exposure=\"none (rule-game only)\" (classroom_round:324), BOOTSTRAP_SOURCE file, corpus_meta.json (sources/author/seed), manifest.json (corpus_sha, ledger_shas, prev_snapshot), parent_mode.json, parent_brief.json prompt_version; train_meta.json records no model/revision/seed (M10); leak_scan has no SCANNER_VERSION; child snapshot hash a09a3545… and package versions now captured under ~/v6_out/ENV (COORD:904). Codex: \"no clean scientific run is authorized until the lineage guard is implemented and regression-tested\"; the manifest must prove exact birth hash, parent-state hash, bootstrap source set, mechanism version, exposure domains, and no ancestor marked QUARANTINE_TASK_EXPOSED, failing closed on anything missing.\n- Build: a provenance module: manifest schema (base id + HF snapshot hash, adapter_config sha, bootstrap corpus sha + its source-ledger shas + the classroom/scanner code versions that produced them, mechanism_version = hash of organism_v6/*.py, writer/compiler path, scanner_version, prompt_versions, exposure_domains, parent-state hash, ancestor chain); admit() that refuses launch on missing fields or quarantined ancestors; write at birth and append at each commit; wire into launch scripts and run_life_v2; negative tests (copied dir without receipts → refuse; self-declared manifest → refuse). Then regenerate an authenticated target-blind bootstrap source: the lineage3_* classroom ledgers behind v3 were produced while classroom/leak-scan defects were live (quarantine doc), so fresh classroom rounds under versioned code are likely required.\n- Time: 1–2 ED guard + tests; fresh classroom source set = several GPU-days (20 lineages × 3 rounds × 8 classrooms was the v3 source scale) + hours of 32B rendering.\n- Judged by: not a measurement — a precondition. Passes when Codex's fail-closed tests pass and the canonical clean-child pointer is set; downstream it makes bootstrap/parenting comparisons scientific_evidence_eligible.\n\nB9. STRUCTURE-HEAVY BOOTSTRAP (Stage 1 adapter pretraining, v2/v3 composition) — PIPELINE 1348; REFINED 1371; DESIGN 1308.\n- Exists: fully built and trained (A7): v3 470 rows (metaflow 206 / opening 94 / contrast 70 / review 65 / full 35), no ritual flags, adapter trained (v2.1, r8, 2 epochs, final loss 0.675); v1 (lived-heavy control) and v2 exist but are permanently task-exposed. Blocked for clean/parented use by the STOP; usable only as development diagnostics. Authored by 32B-AWQ, not the child (M13). Not leak-scanned.\n- Build for the next experiment: (a) B8 first, then regenerate/admit a target-blind source; (b) re-render in the v7 dialect (no DONE, state boundaries, new render_context) because q fields are the old context format — ~half a day of 32B time per 400 episodes; run leak_scan over q/a; (c) controls per IDEAS:1349/COORD:793: none / bootstrap-lived / bootstrap-structure, each ± parent, matched seeds; two-horizon readouts.\n- Time: after the guard, hours to regenerate + minutes–1 h per adapter; the 2×2 at ≥3 seeds = 12 lives ≈ 12 GPUs × ~2 days.\n- Judged by (IDEAS:1372 success criterion): unprompted review/plan text in the first 64 episodes with no parent (needs the B2 review detector; SELF_EVAL/CONTRAST regexes are the current proxies) and later ritual onset than controls (160–224 baseline); plus paired probes, row-specific absorption per sleep (absorption_probe — its chat-masked format matches a v2.1-trained bootstrap but not v1 life adapters, M9), life-level harm, efficiency over cycles (SEQ-005 baseline), echo metric with null.\n\nB10. NEAR-DUPLICATE COLLAPSE IN THE SLEEP CORPUS — DIAGNOSIS 1299/1300; CHECKLIST 1341 (\"deduplication ✗ NOT yet\"); RULED 1390 order of work.\n- Exists: exact-prefix dedup only (sleep_compile.py:147-154; compile_native a[:120]); cumulative prior + new; no recency weighting; no ritual-aware selection; token-set Jaccard helpers exist in parent_brief (_toks, _jaccard). SEQ-006: \"dedup-in-compiler arm … proposed to Codex before build.\" Keeps the compiler organizational (thinker/compiler line).\n- Build: in compile_sleep: near-dup key = content-token Jaccard ≥ θ (or MinHash) against the corpus; collapse to one row with a count (or cap per-template count); log n_collapsed per sleep; optional recency window (conflicts with the docstring's cumulative CLS law — needs a ruling). Trainer unchanged (plain strings). Then an arm vs R3/R5 at matched seeds.\n- Time: 0.5–1 ED + smoke; lives 46 h each.\n- Judged by: ritual onset and persistence (ritual_metrics per 32-episode window; baseline onset 160–224, persists in ~80–100% of later windows); ritual in the weights on probe ledgers (analysis_tables: ON modal share 0.90/0.86, locked 68%/52%); JS first-ACT shift between cycles (0.02–0.13 baseline); late-life harmful lives (R2 4/9); corpus density (rows per sleep, chars per row — IDEAS:1331); paired probes with the per-program table — expected signature of success is the 0.4878/0.5291 plateau dissolving into per-program variance, possibly a lower mean.\n\nB11. PROVISIONAL COMMITS WITH CONFIRMATION — SEQ-002 (ASK→Codex); IDEAS:1380 item 4; RULED 1390 order item 1.\n- Exists: gate verdict is final at sleep (DONE / REJECTED_CANARY / REJECTED_SCORE / REJECTED_BREVITY); latest_adapter = last DONE; --gate-panel mode removed floor decay (floor = max over committed gate probes) but has no confirmation step; sleeps every 32 vs regular probes every 64; motivation measured: same adapter 0.529 → 0.464 (7 replicate-SDs; heavy tail); classroom PREV exam computed but not used in the commit rule.\n- Build: a PROVISIONAL marker that latest_adapter mounts; at the next gate-panel probe (next sleep) or the next regular probe, if ON ≥ floor − tol → DONE, else REJECTED_UNCONFIRMED and revert to the previous DONE (reload model + brief); the floor uses confirmed probes only; wake rows generated under a reverted adapter remain experience (cumulative corpus loses nothing). Also fix classroom_round:360 to require ON ≥ PREV − tol (one line).\n- Time: 0.5–1 ED + smoke; measurable within the running R5 pattern once launched as a new arm (mechanism change to the gate rule → state it in Methods for anything called the freeze recipe).\n- Judged by: gate rejection log by reason incl. reverted provisionals (analysis_tables rejections), same-adapter replicate SD and tail (0.0065; outliers), life-level harm on the untouched report panel, disjoint-panel finals vs base 0.257.\n\nB12. DISJOINT GATE PANEL — review F1 fix; BUILT.\n- Exists: run_life_v2 --gate-panel (see A1); R5_B_seed700 launched (SEQ-006) — the first life whose report numbers are not post-selection; disjoint_panel_v1.json; gate_base probed once at birth; overlap is a hard error. Gap: panel v1 is doing double duty — gate panel for R5 AND the transfer/report panel for the F1b re-probe of R2/R3/R4/RP — so for R5+ lives a source-disjoint REPORT-only panel v2 is needed if both \"held out by identifier\" (8) and \"held out by source\" must be reported unselected; run_probes_batch(eids=None) is hardwired to PROBES. Cost: 12 gate programs × 16 ticks per sleep adds ~12 episodes per sleep (~15% life slowdown).\n- Build: define panel v2 from datasets none of the panels use (poj104-v1, remaining npb/blas with recipe headroom via cgym_eval --ref precheck), add --report-panel; hours CPU. Also fold the three ≤0.034-headroom tensorflow programs decision.\n- Judged by: paired ON−OFF per cycle on the 8-panel and the source-disjoint report panel; life-level harm; gate rejection log; base references (0.257 on v1; -Oz per program from SEQ-003).\n\nB13. FADING SCAFFOLD — DIRECTION 1384 (\"scaffolding faded, absent at deployment\"), 1328; PARENTING_MENU #1 (contingent ladder) and #13 (worked examples fading); M6 red line (\"Parent scaffolds are faded\" is false today).\n- Exists: no fading anywhere — REHEARSAL_TAIL on every brief; the latest parent brief shown at EVERY wake for the rest of life (run_life_v2:143-156, Rohin's repetition ruling IDEAS:1293); parent fires only when ritual is flagged; v3 prompt keeps the same lesson; current lives are parent-present throughout while the binding topology (IDEAS:15-31) says deployment is parent-absent (F4). Tension to reconcile: RULED repetition (the child rehearses) vs DIRECTION fading (the parent's text withdraws) — consistent if the child's rehearsal continues while the parent's text fades.\n- Build: fade schedule in brief() (show the parent brief for W wakes after each brief, or until the echo metric ≥ threshold for two windows, then drop; REHEARSAL_TAIL only early); contingent ladder from admit history (#1, harness-computed); --parent-until <episode> so one life runs parent-present then parent-absent (the retention readout). All prompt/harness level.\n- Time: 0.5–1 ED + lives.\n- Judged by: echo metric with null (echo_null.py: 0.454 after vs 0.354 controls vs 0.322 pre-brief) in windows AFTER withdrawal — does rehearsal persist without the text; ritual windows after withdrawal (existing datum: a context-only lesson lasts ~2 sleeps, RP401 remission then return); paired probes and harm in the parent-absent tail; efficiency markers in the parent-absent phase vs untaught controls at matched exposure.\n\n=====================================================================\nC. SEQUENCING IMPLIED BY THE RULINGS (for the synthesizer)\n=====================================================================\n- Rohin's order (IDEAS:1390): provisional commits → near-duplicate collapse → fading scaffold → v7 stream (no DONE, states, branching) → fail-closed provenance guard for bootstraps. B11, B10, B13 are each ≤1 ED and testable on the existing runner as new arms; B1+B2(+B4) form one v7 lineage root (3–5 ED); B3 rides on B2 (2–3 ED, k× compute); B6 is the largest (3–5 ED) and reuses classroom_round's merge; B8 gates any scientific use of B9 and any parenting comparison; B5 and B7 are mostly panel/curriculum definitions plus small instruments; B12 is done except a report-only panel v2.\n- Reusable pieces: classroom_round (merge, provenance rows, stratified mix, transactional commit, manifest hashing), bootstrap_corpus (five cue paths, acts_ok guard, ritual-on-corpus), probe_adapter (--panel/--gen-seed/--brief-file), efficiency_markers + analysis_tables + echo_null (all CPU from disk), cgym_eval --ref (compiler references), disjoint_panel_v1.json precheck fields (recipe headroom = the persistence-split selector).",

  "The efficiency falsifier for any next-experiment parenting/thinking claim (cycle N, metric, margin, K seeds) must be written before launch; today's instrument shows no taught-vs-untaught separation and declining efficiency in every arm."
 ]
}

{
 "title": "Thinking-first v7: no end token, review-shaped states, recurrent return, and k branches merged by the child — measured by efficiency-over-cycles and ritual against matched v6-stream, text-memory and frozen baselines, before any parenting",
 "design_md": "# Design 2 of 4 — THINKING-FIRST: maximize thinking per situation before any parenting\n\nScope: the experiment that starts after the 09-18 abstract. Nothing here enters the ICLR characterization paper (numbers freeze 09-14 / 09-20). All file references are to `/Users/rohing/dream-state`. All \"current capability\" statements below were checked against `organism_v6/*.py`, `research_notes/EVIDENCE_TABLES.md` (+ addenda), `research_loop/COORDINATION.md` SEQ-001..006, and `research_notes/analysis/efficiency_markers_2026-09-10_{a40,ovx}.json`.\n\n## 0. One paragraph\n\nEvery arm run so far shares one thinking stream: ~13 chunks per program, a child-emitted DONE, no review, no branch, no return to earlier reasoning. On that stream the write locks the birth recipe from cycle 1 (adapter plateau 0.4878 = the birth recipe applied once; -Oz 0.522), ritual sets in at episodes 160–224 in 8/9 unparented lives and persists, chunks-to-best roughly doubles from cycles 2–4 to late life in every arm (R2 3.77±1.05 → 6.6±2.81, n=9; R3 3.94±0.85 → 8.48±1.86, n=6), contrast phrases and backtracks fall to ~0, and the first-ACT distribution stops moving after cycle 1 (JS 0.02–0.13). The parenting arms did not separate from the untaught arms on any of these. The thinking-first hypothesis is that these are properties of the stream the sleep amplifies, not of the write: change the stream (no end token, harness-driven situations, plan/execute/review states with the review pinned into the next state, exact RETURN to earlier states, and k parallel branches merged by the child), hold the writer, compiler, gym, curriculum, gate and budget fixed, and measure whether the first derivative (efficiency over cycles), ritual onset, and behaviour change between cycles move — against a matched v6-stream control, a text-memory agent on the same harness, and the frozen model. Parenting is Stage B and launches only against a stream whose first derivative has been measured, on clean births admitted by a fail-closed guard.\n\n## 1. Hypothesis and what it responds to\n\n**H-thinking (primary).** With writer, compiler, gym, curriculum, gate and per-situation budget held fixed, a stream that (i) never ends by the child's own token, (ii) is organised into review-shaped states whose review conditions the next state, and (iii) can return to and revise earlier states, produces lives whose chunks-to-best does not double over 32 cycles and whose ritual onset is later and less persistent than v6-stream lives. Mechanistically: the sleep is a frequency amplifier of whatever the stream contains (IDEAS 1299–1300, a reading held loosely); a stream with reviews, contrasts and revisions gives it different material.\n\n**H-breadth (secondary).** k parallel branches per situation, merged by the child in a JOIN plan, un-lock the recipe (modal first-action share on the source-disjoint panel falls from the 0.86–0.90 seen ON today) and raise between-cycle behaviour change, at matched total tokens against a single long stream (structure effect) and against the standard budget (total-package effect). Codex's separation of breadth / depth / reflection (COORD 674 item 2) is honoured by giving each its own arm.\n\n**Measured state this answers.** Recipe lock-in (birth recipe 0.4878 = R3 501/503 and R4 600/602/603 plateau; six-pass 0.5291 = R3 500/502; exploring frozen model 0.463–0.488; -Oz 0.522, -O3 0.341); ritual in the weights (probe ledgers ON modal first-action share 0.90/0.86, fully locked 68%/52% of checkpoints, note Jaccard 0.62/0.59 vs OFF 0.50, 0%, 0.105); efficiency decline in every arm (SEQ-005); first disjoint-panel cell R2 seed2 −0.005 to −0.008 vs frozen 0.257; ungated late-life collapse 4/9 lives; gate catches real but post-selection; echo metric 0.454 vs 0.322 vs 0.354; rehearsal claim withdrawn. One fact from the per-life JSON that SEQ-005's table does not show and that matters here: late-life chunks/episode saturates at the 16-tick budget in most lives (R2 seeds 0/4/7/8, R3 503, R4 601/606, RP 402 at 15.4–16.0), so late episodes already end by budget rather than DONE. Consequence: removing DONE changes early life and the probes most; the late-life pathology is not \"stopping early\" but spending 8–9 of 16 chunks before the best action while switching less — thinking that fills the budget without revising. That is exactly what review states and RETURN target.\n\n## 2. Rulings this design binds to, and how each is honoured\n\n- **No end token; boundaries are harness events** (IDEAS 1302; Codex qualifier COORD 674 item 3). DONE removed from `_MARK`, `_MD_MARK`, `bootstrap.txt`, `classify()`, ANCHORS. The harness ends a SITUATION (program switch at the situation budget T, sleep, context reset) and injects `[SITUATION]`; the child may end a STATE (by writing `REVIEW:`), never the stream. CLOCK stays as information. The token-sufficient regime's \"never force consumption\" (IDEAS 312–317) is superseded on this one point by the later no-end-token ruling, which accepts idle-thinking cost explicitly; the budget is the harness's situation length, not a consumption target.\n- **State-to-state reasoning with review-shaped states** (IDEAS 1351): plan → execute → self-determined state end → META-REVIEW (how I evaluated, where wrong, what better, what well, next time, what generalizes); the review is remembered and is the first thing in the next state's context. Built as `REVIEW:` marker + forced review at situation end if none was written + `LAST REVIEW` pinned at the head of the next state. Rohin's deliverable is the text: a review dump he reads every 256 situations is part of the protocol.\n- **Recurrent chain-of-thought** (IDEAS 1399): `RETURN: <state id>` pulls that state's plan and review verbatim into the MIDDLE for revision; the review prompt asks which earlier state it revises; harness-parsed, not regex.\n- **k parallel streams merged by the child; learned search policy, hard cap** (IDEAS 1316, 1359–1360). Version 1 (this experiment): harness-driven k=4 at situation start with a hard cap; the child reads the k branch reviews and writes ONE plan. Version 2 (follow-on arm, only if v1 shows a structure effect): child-emitted `BRANCH n` with n ≤ cap — the allocation-where-a-thought-has-expected-value that Codex names as the learned capability.\n- **Committed-branch-only SLEEP (Codex ASK (a), COORD 674).** My answer, which the design implements: an `ACT` with a gym outcome is a public outcome regardless of branch (CompilerGym and the rule game evaluate each ACT from the program's reset state, so there is no unrealised \"draft action\"); branch rows carry `branch_id`; the compile SEES all branches (Rohin: \"the compile sees all branches; the write carries what survived\") but WRITES only what survived: the best-scoring branch's pathway/wins/recoveries, the child-authored JOIN plan (grounded by the branch outcomes it cites), and the reviews of the surviving branch and the post-JOIN main stream. Unchosen branches' thought, and their reviews, are disposable context (they did their job at JOIN). Dose control: rows selected per situation are capped at the k=1 equivalent, so k raises evidence coverage, not training dose (the analogue of \"renderings raise dose, not evidence\", inverted). M13's open point (IDEAS 1360 vs 64_:188-189) is resolved this way and stated as a design choice, not a ruling.\n- **Sibling-fork vs pooled-clone (Codex ASK (c)).** Stage A has neither: every life is an independent student. Stage B compares matched sibling forks from one clean birth; clones (one child, many classrooms, corpora merged at a sleep barrier, never weights) are Stage C, documented not run. Disposable thought branches are never confused with children.\n- **Conversations → opinions (Codex ASK (b)).** Not in this design; deferred to Stage B+ under Codex's rule (an opinion enters SLEEP only after enactment in a later committed trace).\n- **Thinker/compiler line; compiler dumb** (IDEAS 1293, 331–365). No parent in Stage A. compile_sleep v7 changes are organizational only: grouping by (situation, instance, branch), admitting child-authored review rows grounded by that state's acts, per-situation row cap. Exact dedup as today; near-duplicate collapse is NOT in the root (contingency arm, Codex protocol first).\n- **Token-sufficient regime** (IDEAS 312–317; COORD 979): all arms get the same generous budget within a comparison; probes stay at budget 16 and k=1 for comparability with R2–R5 history; a longer-budget arm and a k=4 probe are their own labelled cells.\n- **Child continuity / new lineage root** (IDEAS 1319; COORD 674 item 4): v7 is a new root; nothing resumes from R2–R5 children (disposable development lives under Codex's firewall, COORD 813).\n- **Contamination and split hygiene** (IDEAS 1346, 1396; quarantine files): Stage A is a mechanism experiment with no parent and no bootstrap, so no parenting comparison is contaminated; its children remain disposable. The mechanism configuration it selects is frozen before Stage B's clean birth, which is the sanctioned one-way path (\"the confirmatory experiment starts again from the sealed clean stage after all mechanisms and policies freeze\"). Report panel v2 is held out by SOURCE and is never a gate panel.\n- **Plasticity deferred; valence cut; no dedup arm, thinking-budget arm, or bootstrapped child without protocol** (IDEAS 1371, 1386; COORD 979): honoured; the budget arm here IS its own arm with probe budget 16.\n- **Repetition vs fading** (IDEAS 1293 vs 1384): moot in Stage A (no parent). Stage B reconciliation proposed: the child keeps rehearsing while the parent's TEXT fades (`--parent-until`, then withdrawal, restored on relapse).\n- **Reporting rules**: cell counts, life as the unit, comparison arm and panel named on every number, \"post-selection\" label wherever gate and report panel coincide (they never do here), no \"sealed\"/\"never seen\".\n\n## 3. Mechanisms built (the v7 stream), each behind a flag so arms ablate cleanly\n\nAll in `organism_v6/`, new root `run_life_v7.py` (a copy of `run_life_v2.py` with the flags below; flags all off must reproduce v6 behaviour byte-for-byte on a replayed ledger — unit test). Every v7 life writes `manifest.json` at birth: base snapshot hash (a09a3545…), `mechanism_version` = sha256 over `organism_v6/*.py`, bootstrap text sha, panel hashes, writer id, flags, seed, curriculum list (persisted at launch and asserted on resume — review minor 8), and appends per commit. This is the provenance RECORD the contamination rule requires of every result; it is not the fail-closed guard (Stage B, section 8).\n\n**M1. `--no-done`.** `batch_loop._MARK` drops DONE; `EpisodeDriver.done` is set only by the harness at `budget_ticks` (= situation length T) or by a sleep/reset. `bootstrap_v7.txt` = `bootstrap.txt` minus the two DONE lines plus three lines (verbatim, so the harness-only cell can price them): \"REVIEW: <text> closes your current state: how you evaluated, where you went wrong, what you did well, what generalizes (and when it does not), what you do next time. Your last review is shown to you at the start of your next state.\" / \"RETURN: <state id> brings an earlier state's plan and review back so you can revise them.\" / \"The workshop moves you to the next program when its time is up. You never stop thinking: if you believe you are done, review, then look for a better route or reflect on what this program taught you.\" The birth-recipe example line is KEPT (changing it would confound the stream comparison; a no-example bootstrap is a separate cell, not run here).\n\n**M2. `--states`.** Ledger rows gain `situation_id`, `state_id`, `parent_state`, `phase`. A state opens at situation start and after each `REVIEW:`; the review text is stored as `kind=\"review\"` with `revises=<state id if named>`; `render_context` gains `STATE k (state j reviewed)` and a `YOUR LAST REVIEW` block (≤ 600 chars) directly under the STATE header; if the situation ends with no review in its last state, the harness injects `[REVIEW] Close this state: write your review now.` and takes the next chunk as the review. Per-state ritual metrics = the frozen 09-09 `ritual_metrics` over the main stream plus one v7-only diagnostic flag, `templated_reviews` (consecutive-review Jaccard ≥ 0.5), reported separately and never folded into the frozen ritual definition.\n\n**M3. `--branches k --branch-horizon E` (harness-driven v1).** At situation start (after the opening chunk), fork k `EpisodeDriver`s sharing the parent's State snapshot and tail; chunk seeds = crc32(f\"{eid}/{tick}/{branch_id}\") ^ gen_seed (the current `_seed_for` would collide across branches). Each branch runs E ticks, is forced to `REVIEW:` at tick E, then a JOIN prompt renders `=== YOUR k BRANCHES ===` (per branch: best action, best score, review) and asks for ONE `NOTE:` plan; the main stream resumes with State merged (best score/action across branches, notes union capped at 10, surprises last 4) and runs the remaining T ticks. vLLM batching: wake batch 8 × k=4 = 32 prompts per call, so wall-clock is ~1.5–1.8× a k=1 life, not 4× (to be calibrated in the smoke run and stated). Gym evaluations rise k× in the branch phase (~0.5–1 s each; generation dominates).\n\n**M4. `--recall-state` (recurrent CoT).** `Ledger.recall_state(state_id)` exact retrieval of that state's plan (first NOTE) and review into MIDDLE when the child writes `RETURN:`; the `LAST REVIEW` pin from M2; harness-parsed counters: states/situation, reviews/situation, RETURNs/situation, `revises` references, JOINs.\n\n**M5. Gate v7 (all v7 arms and the v6-stream control).** `--gate-panel disjoint_panel_v1.json` as in R5 (base measured once at birth, floor = max(gate base, best committed gate probe), overlap with report panels a hard error) plus: (a) behaviour canary redefined for a stream whose chunk count is constant — candidate ACTs/situation ≥ 0.5 × base AND distinct ACT strings/situation ≥ 0.5 × base AND paired parseable-ACT rate ON ≥ OFF − 0.10 (the paired format canary proposed at COORD 539 and never built), replacing chunks/episode ≥ 0.5 × base; (b) `--provisional-commit`: a committed candidate mounts as PROVISIONAL and is confirmed at the next sleep's gate probe (ON ≥ floor − tol) or reverted (REJECTED_UNCONFIRMED; wake rows generated under it stay in the cumulative corpus). (b) is Fable's SEQ-002 proposal awaiting Codex; default ON unless Codex rules otherwise by 09-17 (section 10).\n\n**M6. Compiler `compile_sleep_v7`.** `select()` groups acts by (situation_id, instance, branch_id); for branched situations only the best-scoring branch plus the post-JOIN main stream contribute wins/recoveries/contrasts; the JOIN NOTE and the surviving branch's and main stream's reviews enter as child-authored exemplars (`\"[review] Program X, state k: <review>\"`), grounded by that state's acts; per-situation row cap = what the same function selects from a k=1 situation; exact-prefix dedup unchanged; `n_rows_per_situation`, `n_reviews`, `n_joins` logged per sleep. Flags off ⇒ identical corpus bytes to `compile_sleep` on an archived R2 ledger (unit test).\n\n**M7. Writer v1.1 = v1 recipe + receipts.** Same bare-text LM loss, lr 1e-4, 3 epochs, max_length 512, rank 8, clean-base cumulative; adds `--seed` (manual_seed) and records model id/snapshot/seed in `train_meta.json` (review M10). Recipe unchanged from every life so far, so A0 vs A1 differs only in the stream; the seed fixes reproducibility only. Rows truncated at 512 tokens are counted per sleep; if > 20% of review rows truncate, that is the recorded reason to open an R8 root on the v2.1 masked writer later (Codex's question at COORD 810), not a mid-experiment swap.\n\n**M8. Hygiene fixes bundled into the root (state in Methods):** `model_backend.__call__` forwards `seed` (sleep THINK-calls seeded); wall-clock `alive Ns` frozen to a constant in probe prompts; `get_training_programs` list persisted at launch; the migrate script carries gate/parent flags.\n\n**M9. Instruments.** `efficiency_markers_v7.py` (per situation: chunks-to-best, chars-to-best, ACTs, distinct ACTs, backtracks, states, reviews, RETURNs, JOINs, branch divergence = SD of branch best scores and fraction of situations with ≥ 2 distinct branch first-ACTs; per 32-window: frozen ritual metrics on the main stream, JS shift of first-ACT distribution, `templated_reviews`); `review_dump.py` (20 seeded reviews per life per 256 situations → `research_notes/reviews/`); `analysis_tables.py` extended to two report panels, harm per panel, gate log incl. UNCONFIRMED reverts. All CPU from disk.\n\n**M10. Panel v2 builder (CPU on a node with the cgym venv).** `cgym_eval --list-benchmarks` over datasets none of the panels or curriculum use (poj104-v1, anghabench-v1, github-v0, linux-v0, clgen-v0, csmith-v0), `--ref` and the birth-recipe precheck per program; rule: keep programs with (-Oz score − birth-recipe score) ≥ 0.10 and -Oz score ≥ 0.15 and no name-token overlap with the 67-program curriculum or either existing panel; 16 programs; frozen with a hash before launch; never a gate panel. This is the panel on which situation-dependent choice can register as score (IDEAS 1380 item 3).\n\nNot built for Stage A: near-duplicate collapse, fading, conversations, plasticity, clones, model-driven BRANCH (v2), bootstrap. Each is listed with its trigger in section 10/11.\n\n## 4. Arms and baselines (Stage A)\n\nCommon to every arm: Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct child (snapshot a09a3545…), LoRA rank 8 from birth, writer v1.1, compile_sleep_v7, CompilerGym llvm-v0 with the existing curriculum (cbench/chstone/mibench minus the 8 probes; unchanged so the stream is the only variable — the sibling issue is carried by panel v2, not fixed in the curriculum), 1024 situations, sleep every 32, gate v7 on panel v1, report probes every 64 ON and OFF on the 8-panel and on panel v2 (budget 16, seed 777, k=1, v7 bootstrap for v7 arms), no parent, no bootstrap adapter. Seeds 800–803 are shared ACROSS arms so program order is identical per seed: every cross-arm comparison is paired by seed.\n\n| arm | stream | T (situation chunks) | k × E | writes | seeds | role |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| A0 | v6 (DONE allowed, no states) | 16 | – | yes | 800–802 | matched v6-stream control under v7 hygiene; R5_B_seed700+ are unmatched historical bridges |\n| A1 | v7 core: no-DONE + states + review pin + RETURN | 16 | 1 | yes | 800–803 | reflection + recurrence at standard budget (primary arm) |\n| A1L | v7 core | 32 | 1 | yes | 800–802 | depth: the matched-token control for A2 (16 + 4×4 = 32 chunks) |\n| A2 | v7 core + branches | 16 main | 4 × 4, then JOIN | yes | 800–803 | breadth at the total-package budget |\n| A-T | v7 core, text memory only | 16 | 1 | NO adapter; compile_sleep runs and the waking brief is shown; RECALL over the life ledger | 800–801 | the \"equally capable text-memory agent\" on the same harness (IDEAS 1390; review M4) |\n\nBaselines per life: adapter-OFF (frozen model, same bootstrap, paired at every probe); OFF+brief (brief-only proxy per `probe_adapter --brief-file`, post hoc on both panels at mid-life and end of life); A-T (text agent, whole life); the harness-only cell A1-OFF vs A0-OFF at ep 0/64/128 (prices the bootstrap-text and no-DONE change on the frozen model); fixed references (birth recipe once, -Oz, -O3 on both report panels, CPU). A2 additionally probes panel v2 with k=4 every 128 situations, labelled \"k=train probe\", secondary.\n\n16 lives = 16 GPUs (two 8×A40 nodes). Fallback if only 8 GPUs are granted: A0×2, A1×3, A2×3 (drop A1L and A-T; the structure-vs-tokens question is then unanswered and said so; the text baseline falls back to the brief-only proxy).\n\n## 5. Gyms, panels, traits\n\n- **The gym is the world** (IDEAS 1390): CompilerGym stays. Training split = the existing curriculum. Report panels: the 8 cbench programs (held out by identifier; siblings in the curriculum — labelled as such, kept for continuity with R2–R5) and panel v2 (held out by source, section 3 M10). Gate panel: disjoint panel v1 (12 programs; the three tensorflow programs with ≤ 0.034 -Oz headroom are dropped from the GATE panel if the F1b re-probe shows their ON−OFF SD < 0.005 across adapters — decision 09-17; report panels unaffected).\n- **Trait measured in Stage A (persistence, un-taught):** attempts after a non-improving outcome (backtracks), distinct ACTs per situation, chunks-to-best, and — new under no-DONE — what the child does after its own review when the harness has not moved on (continued changing attempts vs idle repetition). These are exactly the exploit-gym measurements Rohin listed (IDEAS 1402); here they are readouts of the stream, not of a parent.\n- **Trait gyms are Stage B.** Persistence split for Stage B's curriculum: programs where the birth recipe has ≤ +0.02 over the frozen model but -Oz has ≥ 0.10 headroom, drawn from sources disjoint from both report panels (precheck fields already exist in `disjoint_panel_v1.json`); rule-game compound rules for the target-blind classroom. Parenting = trait gyms + environmental feedback + a critic that never gives answers (IDEAS 1405), with the pattern-parent v3 bytes frozen before assignment if it is the critic used.\n\n## 6. Measurement protocol\n\nUnit = life. Cycle = one sleep (32 situations). Always named: comparison arm, panel, budget, k.\n\n1. **Paired probes** every 64 situations: ON then OFF, 8-panel and panel v2, budget 16, seed 777, k=1. Harm = pair < −0.03. Life-level counts (lives with ≥ 1 and ≥ 2 harmful pairs) per panel. Same-adapter replicate SD is 0.0065 but heavy-tailed (one 0.065 pair), so single probes never decide anything; falsifiers use means over 3 probes.\n2. **Efficiency over cycles (first derivative, headline):** chunks-to-best and chars-to-best per situation, per 32-window; per life the ratio ER = mean(cycles 30–32) / mean(cycles 2–4) (interim: cycles 14–16 / 2–4). v6 baselines from disk: R2 pooled 3.77 → 6.6, R3 3.94 → 8.48; per-life ratios spread 0.57–3.2, hence medians and seed-pairing.\n3. **Behaviour-change gate:** JS shift of the first-ACT distribution between consecutive cycles (v6: 0.02–0.13; per-life medians 0.00–0.23).\n4. **Ritual:** frozen 09-09 definition (modal first-ACT ≥ 0.7; PREDICT SD < 0.05 with ≥ 8; consecutive first-NOTE Jaccard ≥ 0.5; modal RECALL ≥ 0.7; ≥ 2 flags), no rehearsal exemption (no parent), on the main stream; onset window and fraction of post-onset windows flagged (v6: onset 160–224 in 8/9; 80–100% persistence). `templated_reviews` reported alongside as a v7 diagnostic.\n5. **Recipe lock in the weights:** modal first-action share and fully-locked fraction on probe ledgers ON vs OFF, per panel (v6 ON 0.90/0.86; OFF 0.50).\n6. **Harness-parsed metacognition counters** (primary): states, reviews, RETURNs, `revises` references, JOINs per situation; branch divergence. Regex markers (self-eval, contrast) secondary, with the SEQ-005 noise caveat.\n7. **Gate log:** commits, rejections by reason (CANARY / SCORE / BEHAVIOUR / UNCONFIRMED), floor history.\n8. **Text-memory comparisons:** A1 ON vs A-T (same harness, whole life) on panel v2; per-life ON vs OFF vs OFF+brief vs ON+brief (proxy).\n9. **Review reader (Rohin's deliverable):** 20 seeded reviews per life at situations 256/512/768/1024 dumped to `research_notes/reviews/`; a 32B-judge rubric (situation-specific? names a wrong belief? states scope? proposes a next-time change?) with prompt version logged, reported as a diagnostic only (IDEAS 219–226: LLM-judge is weak evidence).\n10. **Harness-only cell:** A1-OFF vs A0-OFF at ep 0/64/128 (frozen model under the two harnesses); all learning claims are within-arm ON−OFF.\n11. **Learning-vs-lifetime curves** for every metric, so \"not yet\" is distinguishable from \"no\" (horizon warning, IDEAS 294–305).\n\n## 7. Falsifiers (cycle N, metric, margin, K seeds) — written before launch, frozen in COORD with a SEQ number\n\n- **F-A1 (efficiency arrest, primary).** At cycle 32 (interim cycle 16): median ER over A1 seeds ≤ 1.3 AND ER(A1) < ER(A0) in 3 of 3 matched seed pairs (800–802). K = 4 A1 seeds, 3 A0 seeds. v6 reference ER 1.75–2.15 pooled. If median ER(A1) ≥ 1.6 or A1 loses ≥ 2 pairs, the v7 core does not arrest the efficiency decline at 1024 situations (reported with the horizon caveat; triggers section 10 D7).\n- **F-A2 (ritual).** First ritual window (frozen definition) at situation > 256 in ≥ 3 of 4 A1 seeds AND ritual present in < 50% of windows after onset; else the stream does not delay or reduce ritual (v6: 160–224 in 8/9, 80–100% persistence).\n- **F-A3 (breadth: structure effect at matched tokens).** At cycle 32, A2 vs A1L over 3 matched seeds: modal first-action share ON on panel-v2 probes ≤ 0.6 in A2 in ≥ 2 of 3 pairs while A1L is ≥ 0.8, OR mean paired ON−OFF on panel v2 over cycles 24–32 higher in A2 by ≥ 0.02 in 3 of 3 pairs. Else k=4 adds nothing over a single stream of the same length.\n- **F-A4 (breadth: total package).** A2 vs A1 (4 pairs): mean paired ON−OFF on panel v2 over cycles 24–32 higher in A2 by ≥ 0.02 in ≥ 3 of 4 pairs AND median JS shift between cycles ≥ 0.15 (v6 0.02–0.13). Else \"more tokens, not structure\" (D3 lesson, COORD 674).\n- **F-A5 (no harm).** Any v7 arm in which ≥ 2 of 4 lives have ≥ 2 harmful pairs on panel v2 is harmful under the gate → stop that arm; reference R2 ungated 4/9 lives with ≥ 1 harmful pair.\n- **F-A6 (weights vs text).** Mean ON on panel v2 over cycles 24–32 ≥ OFF+brief + 0.01 in ≥ 3 of 4 A1 seeds, and A1 ON ≥ A-T + 0.02 in 2 of 2 matched pairs; else the gain lives in the text and every weights sentence is dropped.\n- **F-A7 (harness-only).** If |A1-OFF − A0-OFF| ≥ 0.02 on the 8-panel at ep 0, the harness effect is reported as its own row and subtracted from no cross-arm ON comparison (within-arm ON−OFF only).\n- **Un-lock signature expected if H-thinking is right** (stated so a \"success\" cannot be re-read post hoc): panel-v2 ON−OFF gains per program spread across programs rather than two carriers (v6: patricia/gsm carry the 8-panel gain; five programs slightly negative); mean may be LOWER than v6's recipe plateau on the 8-panel and that is not a failure of the hypothesis.\n\n## 8. Stage B — parenting on the v7 stream (contingent; design fixed now, launch gated)\n\n**Preconditions (all):** Stage A readout at cycle 32; Codex's fail-closed ancestry guard implemented with negative tests (copied directory without receipts → refuse; self-declared manifest → refuse; any ancestor QUARANTINE_TASK_EXPOSED or unknown → refuse); a clean target-blind birth admitted through it. The v7 configuration used is the one Stage A selects (A1 if F-A1/F-A2 pass and F-A3/F-A4 fail; A2 if F-A3 or F-A4 pass; the `templated_reviews` and harm rows are tie-breakers), and its bytes freeze before any Stage B child is born.\n\n**Births.** Bootstrap source must be regenerated: fresh rule-game classroom rounds under versioned code (the v3 `lineage3_*` ancestry is unauthenticated; v1/v2 are task-exposed and permanently unusable), rendered in the v7 dialect (the existing corpora's `q` fields are the old `render_context` format), leak-scanned (bootstrap renderings are not scanned today, review minor 7), ritual-measured on the corpus, trained once with v2.1 masked r8 (the bootstrap write is not the life writer; Codex's ASK at COORD 791 stands). Composition v2 (META-FLOW 0.4 / REVIEW 0.15 / OPENING 0.2 / CONTRAST 0.15 / full 0.1). Controls: no bootstrap vs structure bootstrap (the lived-heavy control is task-exposed and cannot be reused; a target-blind lived-heavy control is generated from the same classroom source if budget allows).\n\n**Arms.** 2×2 {bootstrap: none, structure} × {parent: absent, present until situation 512 then withdrawn} × 3 matched seeds = 12 lives, all forks from the same clean birth per bootstrap cell, on the v7 stream, in CompilerGym's training split enriched with the persistence split (same curriculum in all four cells), panel v2 held out by source, gate on panel v1, parent = pattern parent v3 (14B) with bytes frozen before assignment, target-blind on panel v2, text shown at wake while active and withdrawn after 512 (restored on relapse only if that rule is precommitted). Parent servers: 2 GPUs. This is the continual design (parenting and play interleaved) applied to matched forks, which the contamination rule permits; it is not a from-scratch confirmatory run.\n\n**Stage B falsifiers.** F-B1 (parenting, second horizon): mean paired ON−OFF on panel v2 over cycles 24–32 (parent-absent phase) in parent-present forks ≥ parent-absent forks + 0.02 in ≥ 3 of 3 matched seeds AND backtracks per situation in cycles 17–32 ≥ 2× the parent-absent forks; else parenting on the v7 stream shows no retained gain at 1024. F-B2 (bootstrap success criterion, IDEAS 1372): in the first 64 situations with no parent, ≥ 50% of situations contain a child-initiated `REVIEW:` before the harness forces one, in ≥ 2 of 3 bootstrapped seeds vs < 20% in non-bootstrapped; and ritual onset later than the non-bootstrapped siblings in ≥ 2 of 3 pairs. F-B3 (echo with null): first-NOTE overlap with the brief in parent-present forks vs the parent-absent forks' overlap against the same briefs; uptake claims require the Codex criteria (unprompted, different words, changes the next action, survives the 512 withdrawal).\n\n**Stage C (documented, not run):** one child, many clones with a sleep barrier and corpus merge with per-source provenance; evaluator classroom; the killer metric (rate of improvement per unit experience on distributions the parent never showed) is the programme target.\n\n## 9. Compute budget and calendar\n\n**GPU request (submit by 09-15):** two 8×A40 nodes from 09-19 through 10-10 (node 1's lease ends 09-14, node 2's ~09-21; nothing below runs on them). Life throughput ~22 situations/h at k=1, T=16 (~46 h/life); no-DONE adds ≤ 20% early-life generation (late life already fills the budget); T=32 ≈ 1.9×; k=4 branches ≈ 1.5–1.8× wall (batched decode), calibrated in the smoke run.\n\n| item | GPU-h |\n|---|---|\n| new-node setup, smoke (A1, A2, 64 situations + sleep + gate + probe), throughput calibration, panel v2 bases ×3 on both panels | ~100 |\n| A0 × 3 (48 h) | 144 |\n| A1 × 4 (50 h) | 200 |\n| A1L × 3 (90 h) | 270 |\n| A2 × 4 (80 h, incl. k=4 probes) | 320 |\n| A-T × 2 (40 h, no training) | 80 |\n| post hoc: brief-only proxies (16 lives × 2 panels × 2 reps), panel-v2 re-probe of R5 700+ adapters, extra reps on outliers | ~40 |\n| **Stage A total** | **~1150** |\n| Stage B: guard (CPU); classroom source regeneration 8 lineages × 3 rounds × 8 classrooms + parent server (~150); bootstrap render on 32B-AWQ (~6) + adapters (~2) | ~160 |\n| Stage B lives 12 × 55 h (parent overhead) + 2 parent servers × 130 h | ~920 |\n| **Stage B total** | **~1080** |\n| **Grand total** | **~2200** (Stage A alone ~1150) |\n\n**Calendar (day by day; Sat 09-19 start; paper work by others runs in parallel and is untouched):**\n- **09-15–09-18 (pre-work, laptop only, no nodes):** build M1–M10 (~4–5 engineer-days interleaved with the abstract); write the falsifier SEQ entry; Codex asked for rulings due 09-17 (section 10 D5).\n- **Sat 09-19:** new nodes provisioned (`gpu/v2_bootstrap.sh`, cgym venv, model snapshot pinned, ENV captured); unit tests (flags-off byte identity of compile_sleep_v7 vs v6 on an archived R2 ledger; branch-seed determinism; DONE absent from every regex; canary v7 on a synthetic act-once-and-idle ledger); panel v2 built and frozen (hash in COORD).\n- **Sun 09-20:** smoke on 2 GPUs: A1 and A2 for 64 situations, one sleep, gate, both report probes; throughput measured; panel v2 base ×3 and 8-panel base ×3 under the v7 bootstrap (the harness-only cell's OFF reference). Fix day. Paper numbers freeze tonight (unaffected).\n- **Mon 09-21:** launch 16 lives (seeds 800–803 matched). Old node 2 lease ends; its receipts were mirrored earlier.\n- **Tue 09-22:** A-T lives at ~cycle 12, A0/A1 at cycle 8–9: harness-only cell (ep 0/64/128), first gate logs, review dump #1 (situation 256) for Rohin.\n- **Wed 09-23:** A0/A1/A-T reach cycle 16 → interim F-A1/F-A2 read (posted as SEQ); A-T finishes (~40 h); A0 finishes evening.\n- **Thu 09-24:** A1 finishes; brief-only proxies start on freed GPUs; A2/A1L at cycle ~16–20; review dump #2 (512).\n- **Fri 09-25:** paper submitted (others). A2/A1L at cycle ~24–28; panel-v2 re-probe of R5 adapters.\n- **Sat 09-26:** A2/A1L finish (~80–90 h). Stage A cycle-32 readout: F-A1…F-A7 as cell counts, per-program tables, review dumps #3/#4; SEQ entry.\n- **Sun 09-27:** decision meeting (Rohin, Codex, Fable): Stage B go / A1-extension / contingency arm (section 10 D7). Guard code (built 09-22–26 on CPU) submitted to Codex with negative tests.\n- **Mon 09-28–Wed 09-30:** guard review and fixes; classroom source regeneration under versioned code (3 GPU-days with a parent server); bootstrap render + leak scan + ritual-on-corpus; bootstrap adapter; admission through the guard; clean-birth manifest.\n- **Thu 10-01:** Stage B smoke (bootstrapped v7 child, 64 situations, parent on).\n- **Fri 10-02:** Stage B launch (12 lives + 2 parent servers; 2 GPUs for probes).\n- **Sat 10-03–Sun 10-04:** cycle 8 interims; review dumps; echo nulls.\n- **Mon 10-05:** cycle 16 → parent withdrawn at 512 in the parent-present cells; F-B2 interim.\n- **Tue 10-06–Wed 10-07:** lives finish (~55 h); post hoc proxies.\n- **Thu 10-08:** Stage B readout (F-B1–F-B3), SEQ entry.\n- **Fri 10-09:** synthesis and the scale-or-redesign decision (IDEAS 1355): \"aligned but not great\" → Stage C build (clones, evaluator classroom); null → section 11.\n\nElapsed: 21 days (09-19 → 10-09); Stage A alone 8 days.\n\n## 10. Decision points that pending data resolve, and how each outcome changes this design\n\n- **D1. F1b disjoint-panel re-probe (running; first cell R2 seed2 −0.005 to −0.008 vs base 0.257).** (a) If every gated final is ≥ base − 0.03 on panel v1: the abstract may carry a gated-safety sentence; panel v1 is validated as a gate panel and M5 stays as designed. (b) If gated finals are below base − 0.03, or cluster at ~0 transfer like seed2: the 8-panel gain is recipe lock-in with zero transfer; this design's primary score falsifiers already sit on panel v2, and the gate floor on v1 is then effectively base − 0.02, so F-A5's harm count on v2 becomes the safety readout and the abstract carries no gated-safety sentence. (c) If the three tensorflow programs show ON−OFF SD < 0.005 across all adapters, drop them from the gate panel (v1' = 9) before launch; otherwise keep. (d) If panel v1 shows any per-program variance at all (recipe headroom registers), v1 is confirmed as the right SHAPE for v2; if even v1 is flat for every adapter, panel v2's headroom thresholds are raised (≥ 0.15) before freezing.\n- **D2. Text-memory baseline (brief-only proxy; queued, ~5–10 GPU-h).** (a) If OFF+brief ≈ ON for finished lives: the current write carries nothing beyond the brief; the paper drops weights-vs-text; in this design F-A6 becomes the primary falsifier alongside F-A1 (a v7 gain must beat the text agent, not just OFF), and A-T is raised to 3 seeds by dropping one A1L seed. (b) If ON > OFF+brief by ≥ 0.02: weights carry it; F-A6 stays secondary. (c) If the proxy is never run before the node cliff: A-T is the first text baseline the programme has and is reported as such.\n- **D3. R5 (gate on the disjoint panel; seed 700 launched at SEQ-006, zero pairs yet).** (a) If R5's 8-panel ON−OFF and harm match R3's post-selection numbers (0 harmful, means +0.02–0.05): the gate is real; M5 is confirmed as the default for v7 (already designed). (b) If R5 shows harmful pairs on the 8-panel: R3's 0/6 was post-selection; the gate stays (it still refuses collapse shapes) but the abstract limits itself to the rejection log, and provisional commit (M5b) is promoted from default-on to required. (c) If R5's late life shows the R3-500 pattern (all late candidates refused, 0.19–0.34): expected under v6; the v7 arms' late-life rejection counts vs A0 become a reportable cell. (d) The number of R5 lives finished by 09-21 sets how many unmatched bridges exist; A0's three matched seeds do not depend on it.\n- **D4. R4 lives (gate + parent v3, seeds 600–606, young).** (a) If at 1024 and re-probed on v1 R4 separates from R3 on markers (node-1 contrast 5.2 → 10.0 was the only rising marker anywhere) with non-negative v1 finals: \"aligned but not great → scale\" fires for gate+parent; Stage B keeps the pattern parent v3 as the critic (bytes frozen) and its launch priority rises. (b) If R4 does not separate or is harmful on v1: Stage B's critic is redesigned around trait gyms + environmental feedback with the parent as one process-critique channel, and the ritual-triggered brief is demoted to a Stage B ablation. Stage A is unaffected either way — the point of thinking-first is that it does not wait on R4. (c) Conversation phase and v7 trials on RP/R4 children happen only as development tests (Codex firewall), and this design does none.\n- **D5. Codex rulings due 09-17, with defaults if silent.** Gate definition (floor = max over confirmed probes; provisional commit; asymmetric −0.01 margin for replacing PREV): default = provisional commit ON, symmetric tol 0.02; if Codex rules k=2 gate probes instead, M5b becomes a second gate probe (+6 min per sleep). Split hygiene (RULED-PENDING 1396): default = panel v2 held out by source as the only transfer claim; if Codex requires a different test family, panel v2 is replaced before launch (CPU, hours). Writer for the v7 root: default v1.1; if Codex requires v2.1, A0 and all v7 arms switch together (R8 semantics), the historical bridge to R5 is lost and said so. Dedup protocol: not in the root regardless; contingency arm only. Committed-branch-only SLEEP: section 2 is my answer; if Codex rejects branch ACTs as evidence, M6 writes only the post-JOIN main stream and the JOIN note, and A2's dose cap is recomputed.\n- **D6. Provenance guard (Stage B blocker).** Ships only if Codex's negative tests pass; until then no Stage B life, no clean-child pointer. If the guard slips past 10-01, Stage B slides day for day and the freed GPUs run the A1 extension (D7) rather than idling.\n- **D7. Stage A outcomes → next arm.** F-A1 and F-A2 pass, F-A3/F-A4 fail: Stage B on A1; A2 v2 (model-driven BRANCH n ≤ 4) deferred. F-A3 or F-A4 pass: Stage B on A2; A2 v2 becomes the first Stage C mechanism. F-A1 fails with the horizon signature (ER still falling at cycle 32 or reviews still situation-specific by the reader): extend two A1 lives to 2048 situations (continuity rule: mechanisms unchanged) before any parenting. F-A1 fails flat (ritual at 160–224, ER ≥ 1.6, templated reviews): the stream alone is insufficient → contingency arm v7 + near-duplicate collapse (count-capped, Codex protocol first), 4 seeds, before Stage B. F-A5 fails: stop the arm, inspect gate logs, no Stage B on that configuration. F-A7 large: the bootstrap-text delta is priced and a \"v7 harness, v6 bootstrap wording\" cell (2 lives) is added.\n\n## 11. Risks, and what would show this design is wrong\n\n- **Horizon.** 1024 situations may be too short for a stream change to alter the first derivative (IDEAS 294–305). Mitigation: curves not endpoints; D7's 2048 extension; never read a null as mechanism failure without the scale caveat.\n- **Ritual migrates into the reviews.** Templated reviews would satisfy the harness while teaching nothing; `templated_reviews` and the review reader catch it; if reviews template while ACT ritual is delayed, F-A2 \"passes\" for the wrong reason and is reported as such.\n- **Branch collapse.** At temperature 0.7 from an identical context the k branches may choose the same first ACT; branch divergence is measured per situation; if ≥ 80% of situations have identical branch first-ACTs, breadth was not real and F-A3/F-A4 are uninformative (temperature is the exploration knob and is not touched in this experiment; a diversity-prompted branch opening is a harness-side follow-on).\n- **Dose confound in A2.** Handled by the per-situation row cap (M6); the cap is logged and the corpus sizes of A1 and A2 at each sleep are reported side by side.\n- **Bootstrap text change is a treatment.** A1's three added lines are a harness change; F-A7 prices it on the frozen model and the within-arm ON−OFF design keeps learning claims clean.\n- **Writer truncation.** v1.1's 512-token limit may cut long reviews; counted per sleep; the R8 (v2.1) trigger is written down, not improvised.\n- **Gate heavy tails.** A single 8- or 12-episode probe can misjudge an adapter by ~0.06 on rare draws; provisional commit mitigates; floors use confirmed probes only.\n- **Throughput.** k=4 and T=32 lives are 1.5–1.9× longer; if calibration shows > 2×, A1L drops to T=24 and A2 to E=3 with the matched-token identity preserved (16 + 4×3 = 28 ≠ 24 — so both change together to T=28 / E=3), stated before launch.\n- **Compute denial.** 8-GPU fallback in section 4; the breadth question then waits.\n- **Contamination hygiene.** Stage A children are disposable; the mechanism selected is frozen before the clean birth; panel v2 is never used for gating or curriculum; the parent in Stage B never sees panel v2. Every result names its child's manifest.\n- **What would make the thinking-first thesis wrong.** A1 arrests nothing while A-T (text only, same harness) matches A1's scores — the harness, not the learning, did the work; or A1 arrests the efficiency decline but ritual and recipe lock are unchanged on panel v2 — the stream changed how the child spends chunks without changing what it learns; or a later dedup arm arrests ritual with the v6 stream — the compiler, not the stream, was upstream. Each is a reportable outcome and the design's cells distinguish them.\n\n## 12. Build list and ownership\n\nFable builds M1–M10 and the two runners (`run_life_v7.py`, `probe_adapter` k/bootstrap flags), writes the falsifier SEQ entry and the manifest schema, and does not touch GPU nodes until 09-19. Codex rules on D5 by 09-17, reviews the guard's negative tests, and audits the cycle-32 readout number-to-source. Rohin reads the review dumps at 256/512/768/1024 (the deliverable he asked to SEE), rules on the Stage B critic (D4) and on whether \"sleep converges branches\" is satisfied by best-branch survival (section 2). Quantities: ~5 engineer-days before launch, ~2200 GPU-h over 21 days (Stage A ~1150 GPU-h over 8 days), 16 GPUs.",
